Theorem 213.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Every convergence analysis deformation of the core object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 511. The moduli space has dimension at most 111 with 3 connected components.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_511(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 511. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^111(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 213.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on core structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 579. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 143 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the core space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 579 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 143. QED.

Theorem 213.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Let the core structure S satisfy the conditions of optimality condition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 704. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 689 and converges at rate O(1/n^1).

Proof: We apply the optimality condition principle to the core decomposition. By minimality, the element a^704 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 689-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 213.6 (Bound Derivation)

Statement: Let ρ be a bound derivation functional on the core space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 168 and the convergence rate is O(184/n^1).

For the inductive step, we decompose the core structure using the bound derivation theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 184.
